<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 270C. DEPARTMENT OF REVENUE</h2>
      
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL POWERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.01">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.01 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this chapter only, the following words, terms, and phrases have the meanings given them in this section unless the language or context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of revenue or a person to whom the commissioner has delegated functions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Department.</span></h2>
          <p>"Department" means the Department of Revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Electronic means; electronically.</span></h2>
          <p>"Electronic means" and "electronically" mean a method that is electronic, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325L.02">325L.02, paragraph (e)</a>, and that is prescribed by the commissioner. Electronic means includes the use of a touch-tone telephone to transmit return information in a manner prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Law administered by the commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Law administered by the commissioner" means a law or rule that vests or imposes a power, duty, responsibility, or authority in the commissioner, except the following laws: (1) the property tax laws, and (2) chapter 16D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means an individual, trust, estate, fiduciary, partnership, company, corporation, limited liability company, association, governmental unit or agency, public or private organization of any kind, or other legal entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Property tax laws.</span></h2>
          <p>"Property tax laws" means all laws and rules related to the administration of the tax on property referred to in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.01#stat.272.01.1">272.01, subdivision 1</a>, and all laws related to the administration of the tax on wind energy production imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.029#stat.272.029.1">272.029, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Return.</span></h2>
          <p>"Return" means a return, information return, or report, required by a law administered by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">State revenue laws.</span></h2>
          <p>"State revenue laws" means all laws administered by the commissioner and the property tax laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Tax.</span></h2>
          <p>"Tax" means a tax or fee imposed by a law administered by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.01.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Taxpayer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Taxpayer" means a person subject to, or liable for, a tax or fee imposed by a law administered by the commissioner; a person required to file a return, information return, or report, with respect to, or to pay, or withhold or collect and remit, a tax or fee imposed by a law administered by the commissioner; a person required to obtain a license or a permit under a law administered by the commissioner; or a person required to keep records regarding a tax or fee imposed by a law administered by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.1.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/259/#laws.8.1.0">2006 c 259 art 8 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.02">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.02 DEPARTMENT OF REVENUE; COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.02.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner; supervision of department and appointment.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Revenue is under the supervision and control of the commissioner. The commissioner shall be appointed by the governor under the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.06">15.06</a>. The commissioner shall be selected on the basis of ability and experience in the field of tax administration and without regard to political affiliations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.02.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Power to appoint staff.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may organize the department as the commissioner deems necessary, and appoint one deputy commissioner, a department secretary, directors of divisions, and such other officers, employees, and agents, as the commissioner deems necessary to carry out the duties, responsibilities, and authority entrusted to the commissioner. The commissioner may define the duties of such officers, employees, and agents, and delegate to them any of the commissioner's powers or duties, subject to the commissioner's control and under such conditions as the commissioner may prescribe. Appointments to exercise delegated power to sign documents which require the signature of the commissioner or a delegate by law shall be by written order filed with the secretary of state. The delegations of authority granted by the commissioner remain in effect until revoked by the commissioner or a successor commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may appoint agents as the commissioner considers necessary to make examinations and determinations. The agents have the rights and powers conferred on the commissioner to subpoena, examine, and copy books, records, papers, or memoranda, subpoena witnesses, administer oaths and affirmations, and take testimony.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.02.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Salaries.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall appoint and employ additional employees and other agents, purchase supplies or materials, or incur other expenditures in the administration and enforcement of state revenue laws as considered necessary. The salaries of all agents and employees provided for in this chapter shall be fixed by the appointing authority, subject to the approval of the commissioner of management and budget.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.02.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Office and supplies furnished; expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall be provided with suitable and necessary office furniture, supplies, stationery, books, periodicals, newspapers, maps, and financial and commercial reports; and all necessary expenses therefor shall be audited and paid as other expenses are audited and paid. The actual necessary expenses of the commissioner, the commissioner's employees and agents, and such experts and assistants as may be employed by the commissioner while traveling on the business of the department, shall be paid by the state. The expenditures must be sworn to by the party who incurred the expense and approved by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.02.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Filing officers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner is the filing officer and custodian of the books, files, and records of the department. The commissioner may certify copies of the books, files, and records in the custody of the commissioner for all purposes in the same manner as other custodians of public records. The commissioner may authorize other employees of the department to certify books, files, and records in the custody of the commissioner. The authorization must be made by a written order stating the documents that may be certified and must be filed with the secretary of state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.02.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Department seal.</span></h2>
          <p>The department shall have a seal engraved with the words, "State of Minnesota, Department of Revenue." Such seal may be used to authenticate the official acts of the commissioner or any other employees of the department, but the failure to use the seal shall not invalidate any such acts. Duplicate seals may be provided for the use of directors of divisions or other employees of the department.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.02.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.2.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.11.1.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 11 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.42.0">2008 c 204 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.03">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.03 POWERS AND DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.03.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall have and exercise the following powers and duties:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) administer and enforce the assessment and collection of taxes;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) make determinations, corrections, and assessments with respect to taxes, including interest, additions to taxes, and assessable penalties;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) use statistical or other sampling techniques consistent with generally accepted auditing standards in examining returns or records and making assessments;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) investigate the tax laws of other states and countries, and formulate and submit to the legislature such legislation as the commissioner may deem expedient to prevent evasions of state revenue laws and to secure just and equal taxation and improvement in the system of state revenue laws;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) consult and confer with the governor upon the subject of taxation, the administration of the laws in regard thereto, and the progress of the work of the department, and furnish the governor, from time to time, such assistance and information as the governor may require relating to tax matters;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) execute and administer any agreement with the secretary of the treasury or the Bureau of Alcohol, Tobacco, Firearms and Explosives in the Department of Justice of the United States or a representative of another state regarding the exchange of information and administration of the state revenue laws;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) require town, city, county, and other public officers to report information as to the collection of taxes received from licenses and other sources, and such other information as may be needful in the work of the commissioner, in such form as the commissioner may prescribe;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) authorize the use of unmarked motor vehicles to conduct seizures or criminal investigations pursuant to the commissioner's authority;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) authorize the participation in audits performed by the Multistate Tax Commission. For the purposes of chapter 270B, the Multistate Tax Commission will be considered to be a state for the purposes of auditing corporate sales, excise, and income tax returns;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) maintain toll-free telephone access for taxpayer assistance for calls from locations within the state; and</p>
          <p class="in">(11) exercise other powers and authority and perform other duties required of or imposed upon the commissioner by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.03.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Mission; efficiency.</span></h2>
          <p>It is part of the department's mission that within the department's resources the commissioner shall endeavor to:</p>
          <p>(1) prevent the waste or unnecessary spending of public money;</p>
          <p>(2) use innovative fiscal and human resource practices to manage the state's resources and operate the department as efficiently as possible;</p>
          <p>(3) coordinate the department's activities wherever appropriate with the activities of other governmental agencies;</p>
          <p>(4) use technology where appropriate to increase agency productivity, improve customer service, increase public access to information about government, and increase public participation in the business of government;</p>
          <p>(5) utilize constructive and cooperative labor-management practices to the extent otherwise required by chapters 43A and 179A;</p>
          <p>(6) report to the legislature on the performance of agency operations and the accomplishment of agency goals in the agency's biennial budget according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.10#stat.16A.10.1">16A.10, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
          <p>(7) recommend to the legislature appropriate changes in law necessary to carry out the mission and improve the performance of the department.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.03.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.3.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/253/#laws.0.14.0">2006 c 253 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.48.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.13.7.0">2013 c 143 art 13 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.04">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.04 USE OF INFORMATION.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of any other law, the commissioner may use any and all information in the commissioner's possession, or to which the commissioner has access, to insure equal and consistent application and enforcement of all state revenue laws. This section shall not be construed as granting to the commissioner any power to release any information outside the department.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.04.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.4.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.05">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.05 ACCESS TO CRIMINAL JUSTICE DATA.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may enter into an agreement with the commissioner of public safety to allow designated employees of the Department of Revenue to have access to the criminal justice data communications network provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/299C.46">299C.46</a>. For purposes of that section, the criminal investigation unit of the Department of Revenue is considered a criminal justice agency.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.5.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.055">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.055 CRIMINAL INVESTIGATIONS, REFERRAL, AND INFORMATION DISCLOSURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.055.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requesting assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner has reason to believe that a criminal violation of the state revenue laws or chapter 349 has occurred, the commissioner may request the attorney general or the prosecuting authority of any county to assist in a criminal investigation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.055.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Referral for prosecution.</span></h2>
          <p>If a proceeding is referred to a prosecuting authority, and the prosecuting authority fails to issue or cause to be issued an indictment or criminal complaint within 30 days after the referral by the commissioner, the attorney general may conduct the proceeding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.055.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Authority to disclose information.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may disclose information to the prosecuting authority and attorney general pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270B.05#stat.270B.05.2">270B.05, subdivision 2</a>, clause (1).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.055.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.6.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.06">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.06 RULEMAKING AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall, from time to time, make, publish, and distribute rules for the administration and enforcement of state revenue laws. The rules have the force of law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.7.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.07">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.07 REVENUE NOTICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.07.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may make, adopt, and publish interpretive revenue notices. A "revenue notice" is a policy statement that has been published pursuant to subdivision 5 and that provides interpretation, details, or supplementary information concerning the application of state revenue laws or rules promulgated by the commissioner. Revenue notices are published for the information and guidance of taxpayers, local government officials, the department, and others concerned.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.07.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effect.</span></h2>
          <p>Revenue notices do not have the force and effect of law and have no precedential effect, but may be relied on by taxpayers until revoked or modified. A notice may be expressly revoked or modified by the commissioner, by the issuance of a revenue notice, but may not be revoked or modified retroactively to the detriment of the taxpayers. A change in the law or an interpretation of the law occurring after the revenue notice is issued, whether in the form of a statute, court decision, administrative rule, or revenue notice, results in revocation or modification of the notice to the extent that the change affects the notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.07.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Retroactivity.</span></h2>
          <p>Revenue notices are generally interpretive of existing law and therefore are retroactive to the effective date of the applicable law provision unless otherwise stated in the notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.07.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Issuance.</span></h2>
          <p>The issuance of revenue notices is at the discretion of the commissioner. The commissioner shall establish procedures governing the issuance of revenue notices and tax information bulletins. At least one week before publication of a revenue notice in the State Register, the commissioner shall provide a copy of the notice to the chairs of the Taxes Committee of the house of representatives and the Taxes and Tax Laws Committee of the senate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.07.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Publication.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall publish the revenue notices in the State Register and in any other manner that makes them accessible to the general public. The commissioner may charge a reasonable fee for publications.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.07.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.8.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.08">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.08 TAX INFORMATION BULLETINS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may issue tax information bulletins. "Tax information bulletins" are informational guides to enable taxpayers and local governmental officials to become more familiar with state revenue laws and their rights and responsibilities under these laws. Nothing contained in the tax information bulletins supersedes, alters, or otherwise changes any provisions of the state revenue laws, administrative rules, court decisions, or revenue notices.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.9.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.085">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.085 NOTIFICATION REQUIREMENTS; SALES AND USE TAXES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of revenue shall establish a means of electronically notifying persons holding a sales tax permit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.84">297A.84</a> of any statutory change in chapter 297A and any issuance or change in any administrative rule, revenue notice, or sales tax fact sheet or other written information provided by the department explaining the interpretation or administration of the tax imposed under that chapter. The notification must indicate the basic subject of the statute, rule, fact sheet, or other material and provide an electronic link to the material. Any person holding a sales tax permit that provides an electronic address to the department must receive these notifications unless they specifically request electronically, or in writing, to be removed from the notification list. This requirement does not replace traditional means of notifying the general public or persons without access to electronic communications of changes in the sales tax law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.085.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.4.1.0">2009 c 88 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.18.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.09">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.09 OPINION OF ATTORNEY GENERAL; EFFECT.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may in writing request the opinion of the attorney general upon any matter regarding the state revenue laws. Any written opinion of the attorney general upon any such matter rendered in response to such request shall have the force and effect of law unless and until overruled by a decision of the Tax Court or a court of competent jurisdiction.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.10.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.10">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.10 EX-EMPLOYEES NOT TO REPRESENT TAXPAYERS; PENALTY.</h1>
        <p>An employee of the department may not, for a period of one year after the employee's employment has terminated, act as counsel, attorney, or agent for a taxpayer in connection with a claim or proceeding pending in the department. An employee of the department may not act as counsel, attorney, or agent for a taxpayer at any time after termination of employment in connection with a claim or proceeding of which the person has knowledge that was acquired during the term of employment. A violation of this section is a gross misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.11.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.101">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.101 APPLICATION FOR BUSINESS REGISTRATION; CERTAIN INFORMATION NOT REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an entity applying for a Minnesota business tax account number is not required to list the names, home addresses, and Social Security numbers of its officers or directors when the entity applying for an account number is an instrumentality of a state, a local, or the federal government, or a tribal government.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.5.2.0">2011 c 112 art 5 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.105">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.105 BASIS FOR EVALUATION OF DEPARTMENT OF REVENUE EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <p>The department must not use tax enforcement results to impose individual revenue quotas with respect to employees or their immediate supervisors who are directly involved in assessment or collection activities. The department may, however, use individual performance with regard to number of cases completed and, in the case of collections employees, dollars collected, as factors in evaluating an employee and not be considered as failing to comply with this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.105.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.12.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.11">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.11 TAX EXPENDITURE BUDGET.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.11.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Statement of purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>State governmental policy objectives are sought to be achieved both by direct expenditure of governmental funds and by the granting of special and selective tax relief or tax expenditures. Both direct expenditures of governmental funds and tax expenditures have an effect on the ability of the state and local governments to lower tax rates or to increase expenditures. As a result, tax expenditures should receive a regular and comprehensive review by the legislature as to (1) their total cost, (2) their effectiveness in achieving their objectives, (3) their effect on the fairness and equity of the distribution of the tax burden, and (4) the public and private cost of administering tax expenditure financed programs. This section is intended to facilitate a regular review of the state and local tax expenditure budget by the legislature by providing for the preparation of a regular biennial tax expenditure budget.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.11.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Preparation; submission.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prepare a tax expenditure budget for the state. The tax expenditure budget report shall be submitted to the legislature by November 1 of each even-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.11.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Period covered.</span></h2>
          <p>The report shall include estimates of annual tax expenditures for, at a minimum, a three-year period including the two-year period covered in the governor's budget submitted in the preceding January pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.11">16A.11</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.11.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The report shall detail for each tax expenditure item:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount of tax revenue forgone;</p>
          <p>(2) a citation of the statutory or other legal authority for the expenditure;</p>
          <p>(3) the year in which it was enacted or the tax year in which it became effective;</p>
          <p>(4) the purpose of the expenditure, as identified in the enacting legislation in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.192">3.192</a> or by the Tax Expenditure Review Commission;</p>
          <p>(5) the incidence of the expenditure, if it is a significant sales or income tax expenditure; and</p>
          <p>(6) the revenue-neutral amount by which the relevant tax rate could be reduced if the expenditure were repealed.</p>
          <p>(b) The report may contain additional information which the commissioner considers relevant to the legislature's consideration and review of individual tax expenditure items. This may include but is not limited to analysis of whether the expenditure is achieving that objective and the effect of the expenditure on the administration of the tax system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.11.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Revenue estimates; legislative bills.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon reasonable notice from the chair of the house of representatives or senate tax committee that a bill is scheduled for hearing, the commissioner shall prepare an estimate of the effect on the state's tax revenues which would result from the passage of a legislative bill establishing, extending, or restricting a tax expenditure. These revenue estimates shall contain the same information as provided in subdivision 4 for expenditure items contained in the tax expenditure budget, as appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.11.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p>(1) "business tax credit" means:</p>
          <p>(i) a credit against the corporate franchise tax claimed by a C corporation; or</p>
          <p>(ii) a credit against the individual or fiduciary income tax claimed by a pass-through entity that is allocated to its partners, members, or shareholders;</p>
          <p>(2) "pass-through entity" means a partnership, limited liability corporation, or S corporation;</p>
          <p>(3) "significant tax expenditure" means a tax expenditure, but excluding any tax expenditure that:</p>
          <p>(i) is incorporated into state law by reference to a federal definition of income;</p>
          <p>(ii) results in a revenue reduction of less than $10,000,000 per biennium; or</p>
          <p>(iii) is a business tax credit;</p>
          <p>(4) "tax expenditure" means a tax provision which provides a gross income definition, deduction, exemption, credit, or rate for certain persons, types of income, transactions, or property that results in reduced tax revenue, but excludes provisions used to mitigate tax pyramiding;</p>
          <p>(5) "tax" means any tax of statewide application or any tax authorized by state law to be levied by local governments generally. It does not include a special local tax levied pursuant to special law or to a special local tax levied pursuant to general authority that is no longer applicable to local governments generally; and</p>
          <p>(6) "tax pyramiding" means imposing sales taxes under chapter 297A on intermediate business-to-business transactions rather than sales to final consumers.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.11.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.13.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.11.14.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 11 s 14</a>-16</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.12">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.12 TAX INFORMATION SAMPLE DATA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.12.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Preparation of samples.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prepare microdata samples of income tax returns and other information useful for purposes of:</p>
          <p>(1) estimating state revenues;</p>
          <p>(2) simulating the effect of changes or proposed changes in state and federal tax law on the amount of state revenues; and</p>
          <p>(3) analyzing the incidence of present or proposed taxes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.12.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Coordinating committee.</span></h2>
          <p>A coordinating committee is established to oversee and coordinate preparation of the microdata samples. The committee consists of:</p>
          <p>(1) the director of the Research Division of the department who shall serve as chair of the committee;</p>
          <p>(2) the state economist;</p>
          <p>(3) the chair of the Committee on Taxes of the house of representatives or the chair's designee; and</p>
          <p>(4) the chair of the Committee on Taxes and Tax Laws of the senate or the chair's designee. The committee shall consider the analysis needs and use of the microdata samples by the Management and Budget and Revenue Departments and the legislature in designing and preparing the samples, including the type of data to be included, the structure of the samples, size of the samples, and other relevant factors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.12.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contents of samples.</span></h2>
          <p>The samples must consist of information derived from a random sample of federal and Minnesota individual income tax returns. The samples prepared in odd-numbered years must be augmented by additional information from other sources as the coordinating committee determines is feasible and appropriate. The coordinating committee shall consider inclusion of:</p>
          <p>(1) information derived from property tax refund returns;</p>
          <p>(2) the estimated market value of the taxpayer's home from the homestead declaration; and</p>
          <p>(3) information from other sources, such as the surveys conducted by the United States Departments of Commerce and Labor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.12.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Consultation on analysis models.</span></h2>
          <p>The coordinating committee shall facilitate regular consultation among the Department of Revenue, the Department of Management and Budget, and house of representatives and senate staffs in development and maintenance of their respective computer models used to analyze the microdata samples. The committee shall encourage efforts to attain more commonality in the models, greater sharing of program development efforts and programming tasks, and more consistency in the resulting analyses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.12.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duration.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of any statutes to the contrary, the coordinating committee as established by this section to oversee and coordinate preparation of the microdata samples of income tax returns and other information shall not expire.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.14.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.11.2.0">2009 c 88 art 11 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/286/#laws.8.33.0">2014 c 286 art 8 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.13">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.13 TAX INCIDENCE REPORTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.13.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Biennial report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall report to the legislature on the overall incidence of the income tax, sales and excise taxes, and property tax.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must submit the report:</p>
          <p>(1) by March 1, 2021; and</p>
          <p>(2) by March 1, 2024, and each even-numbered year thereafter.</p>
          <p>(c) The report shall present information on the distribution of the tax burden as follows: (1) for the overall income distribution, using a systemwide incidence measure such as the Suits index or other appropriate measures of equality and inequality; (2) by income classes, including at a minimum deciles of the income distribution; and (3) by other appropriate taxpayer characteristics.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may request information from any state officer or agency to assist in carrying out this section. The state officer or agency shall provide the data requested to the extent permitted by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.13.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bill analyses.</span></h2>
          <p>At the request of the chair of the house of representatives Tax Committee or the senate Committee on Taxes and Tax Laws, the commissioner shall prepare an incidence impact analysis of a bill or a proposal to change the tax system which increases, decreases, or redistributes taxes by more than $20,000,000. To the extent data is available on the changes in the distribution of the tax burden that are affected by the bill or proposal, the analysis shall report on the incidence effects that would result if the bill were enacted. The report may present information using systemwide measures, such as Suits or other similar indexes, by income classes, taxpayer characteristics, or other relevant categories. The report may include analyses of the effect of the bill or proposal on representative taxpayers. The analysis must include a statement of the incidence assumptions that were used in computing the burdens.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.13.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Income measure.</span></h2>
          <p>The incidence analyses shall use the broadest measure of economic income for which reliable data is available.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.13.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.15.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/7/#laws.10.1.0">1Sp2011 c 7 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/3/#laws.0.2.0">2013 c 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.11.17.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 11 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.18.2.0">2023 c 64 art 18 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.270C.131"><b>270C.131</b> MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.23.4.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 23 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.14">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.14 AUTHORITY TO PAY LOCAL TAXES; APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may pay to any local government unit, any locally imposed sales taxes that may be assessed against the department. There is appropriated to the commissioner from the general fund the amount needed to make the payments.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.14.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.16.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.270C.145"><b>270C.145</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.5.15.0">2013 c 142 art 5 s 15</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.15">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.15 REVENUE DEPARTMENT SERVICE AND RECOVERY SPECIAL REVENUE FUND.</h1>
        <p>A Revenue Department service and recovery special revenue fund is created for the purpose of recovering the costs of furnishing government data and related services or products, as well as recovering costs associated with collecting local taxes on sales and the retail delivery fee established under chapter 168E. All money collected under this section is deposited in the Revenue Department service and recovery special revenue fund. Money in the fund is appropriated to the commissioner to reimburse the department for the costs incurred in administering the tax law or providing the data, service, or product. Any money paid to the department as a criminal fine for a violation of state revenue law that is designated by the court to fund enforcement of state revenue law is appropriated to this fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.17.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.3.20.0">2023 c 68 art 3 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.16">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.16 COLLECTION OF DELINQUENT LIABILITIES; COSTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.16.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of collecting delinquent tax liabilities or debts as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16D.02#stat.16D.02.3">16D.02, subdivision 3</a>, there is appropriated to the commissioner an amount representing the cost of collection by contract with collection agencies, revenue departments of other states, or attorneys to enable the commissioner to reimburse these agencies, departments, or attorneys for this service. The commissioner shall report quarterly on the status of this program to the chair of the house of representatives Tax and Appropriation Committees and senate Tax and Finance Committees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.16.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prepayment.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.15#stat.16A.15.3">16A.15, subdivision 3</a>, the commissioner may authorize the prepayment of sheriff's fees, attorney fees, fees charged by revenue departments of other states, or court costs to be incurred in connection with the collection of delinquent tax liabilities owed to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.18.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 18</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.17">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.17 COMMISSIONER TO COLLECT CERTAIN LOCAL TAXES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.17.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Costs deducted; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner agrees to collect a local tax, the local unit of government must agree that all the direct and indirect costs of the department for collecting the tax and any other statewide indirect costs will be deducted from the amounts collected and paid to the local unit of government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.17.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.14.4.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 14 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.17.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.19.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 19</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.171">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.171 SPECIAL LAWS; LOCAL TAXES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.171.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a special law grants a local government unit or group of units the authority to impose a local tax other than sales tax, including but not limited to taxes such as lodging, entertainment, admissions, or food and beverage taxes, and the Department of Revenue either has agreed to or is required to administer the tax, such that the tax is reported and paid with the chapter 297A taxes, then each term used in the special law is defined as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) as defined in chapter 297A or in Minnesota Rules, chapter 8130; or</p>
          <p>(2) if the specific term is not defined either in chapter 297A or in Minnesota Rules, chapter 8130, then defined consistent with the position of the Department of Revenue as to the extent of the tax base.</p>
          <p>(b) This subdivision does not apply to terms that are defined by the authorizing special law.</p>
          <p>(c) This subdivision applies notwithstanding whether a local government unit or group of units adopts consistent definitions into local law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.171.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to a special law that is described in subdivision 1 that was:</p>
          <p>(1) originally enacted prior to 2010, and that was amended by special law in or after 2010, to extend the time for imposing the tax or to modify the tax base; or</p>
          <p>(2) first enacted in or after 2010.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.171.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/389/#laws.4.14.0">2010 c 389 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.19.1.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 19 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.18">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.18 SETOFF OF POLITICAL SUBDIVISION DEBTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) As used in this section, "political subdivision" means counties and home rule charter or statutory cities, and "debts" means a legal obligation to pay a fixed amount of money, which equals or exceeds $100 and which is due and payable to the claimant political subdivision.</p>
        <p>(b) If one political subdivision owes a debt to another political subdivision, and the debt has not been paid within six months of the date when payment was due, the creditor political subdivision may notify the commissioner of the debt, and shall provide the commissioner with information sufficient to verify the claim. If the commissioner has reason to believe that the claim is valid, and the debt has not been paid, the commissioner shall initiate setoff procedures under this section.</p>
        <p>(c) Within ten days of receipt of the notification from the creditor political subdivision, the commissioner shall send a written notice to the debtor political subdivision, advising it of the nature and amount of the claim. This written notice shall advise the debtor of the creditor political subdivision's intention to request setoff of the refund against the debt. The notice will also advise the debtor that the debt can be set off against a state aid payment, and will advise the debtor of the right to contest the validity of the claim at a hearing. The debtor must assert this right by written request to the commissioner, which request the commissioner must receive within 45 days of the mailing date of the notice.</p>
        <p>(d) If the commissioner receives written notice of a debtor political subdivision's intention to contest at hearing the claim upon which the intended setoff is based, the commissioner shall initiate a hearing according to contested case procedures established in the state Administrative Procedure Act not later than 30 days after receipt of the debtor's request for a hearing. The costs of the hearing shall be paid equally by the political subdivisions that are parties to the hearing. The Office of Administrative Hearings shall separately bill each political subdivision for one-half of the costs.</p>
        <p>(e) If the debtor political subdivision does not object to the claim, or does not prevail in an objection to the claim or at a hearing on the claim, the commissioner shall deduct the amount of the debt from the next payment scheduled to be made to the debtor under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1398">273.1398</a> or chapter 477A. The commissioner shall remit the amount deducted to the claimant political subdivision.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.20.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 20</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.19">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.19 TAXES AND FEES; REFUND AND SHARING AGREEMENTS WITH INDIANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.19.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Taxes paid by Indians.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding any other law which limits the refund of tax, the commissioner is authorized to enter into a tax refund agreement with the governing body of any federally recognized Indian Tribe in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(b) The agreement may provide for:</p>
          <p>(1) a mutually agreed-upon amount as a refund to the governing body of an estimate of any sales or excise tax paid by Tribal members on transactions occurring on the reservation or on transactions that would occur on the reservation if there was no agreement; or</p>
          <p>(2) an amount which measures the economic value of an agreement by the Tribal government to pay the equivalent of the state sales tax on items included in the sales tax base but exempt on the reservation.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of this section, "Tribal members" means the number of enrolled members of the Tribe who live on or adjacent to the reservation as defined in the agreement.</p>
          <p>(d) In arriving at the refund amount, the commissioner must consider the number of Tribal members as most recently submitted by the Tribe to the commissioner, estimates contained in the tax incidence report under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.13">270C.13</a>, and any other information available to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.19.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Sales, use, and excise taxes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner is authorized to enter into a tax agreement with the governing body of any federally recognized Indian Tribe in Minnesota, that provides for the state and the Tribal government to share sales, use, and excise tax revenues generated from on-reservation activities of non-Tribal members and off-reservation activities of Tribal members. Every agreement entered into pursuant to this subdivision must require the commissioner to collect all state and Tribal taxes covered by the agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner is authorized to collect any Tribal taxes imposed pursuant to any agreement entered into pursuant to this subdivision and to make payments authorized by the agreement to the Tribal government from the funds collected.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall pay to the Tribal government its share of the taxes collected pursuant to the agreement, as indicated in the agreement, and grant the taxpayer a credit for the taxpayer's share of the amount paid to the Tribal government against the taxpayer's Minnesota tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.19.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>There is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner the amounts necessary to make the refunds provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.19.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Payments to counties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall pay to a county in which an Indian gaming casino is located:</p>
          <p>(1) ten percent of the state share of all taxes generated from activities on reservations and collected under a tax agreement under this section with the tribal government for the reservation located in the county; or</p>
          <p>(2) five percent of excise taxes collected by the state that are determined by the department to have been generated from activities on a reservation located in the county, the tribal government of which does not have a tax agreement under this section and did not have a tax agreement on June 30, 2003.</p>
          <p>If the tribe has casinos located in more than one county, the payment must be divided equally among the counties in which the casinos are located.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall make the payments required under this subdivision by February 28 of the year following the year the taxes are collected.</p>
          <p>(c) An amount sufficient to make the payments authorized by this subdivision is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.19.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Fees; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may enter into an agreement with the governing body of any federally recognized Indian reservation in Minnesota concerning fees administered by the commissioner that are paid by the tribe, members of the tribe, or persons who conduct business with the tribe, or otherwise imposed on on-reservation activities. The agreement may provide for the refund or sharing of the fee. The commissioner may make any payments required by the agreement from the fees collected.</p>
          <p>(b) Each head of an agency, board, or other governmental entity that administers a program that is funded by fees administered by the commissioner may sign an agreement entered into by the commissioner under this subdivision. An agreement is not valid until signed by the head of each agency, board, or other governmental entity that administers a program funded by the particular fee covered in an agreement and by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) There is annually appropriated to the commissioner from the funds for which the fees are collected the amounts necessary to make payments as provided in this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.19.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Cannabis sales.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner is authorized to enter into a tax agreement with the governing body of any federally recognized Indian Tribe in Minnesota that provides for the state and the Tribal government to share state and local sales tax and gross receipts tax imposed on the sale of cannabis at retail by a Tribally owned business at a location off the reservation.</p>
          <p>(b) Any payment to the Tribe under this subdivision must be limited to an approximation of the expenses borne by the Tribe in regulating the production and supplying of cannabis products to the off-reservation retail business and to taxes paid by members of the Tribe at that business location.</p>
          <p>(c) Authority under this subdivision applies only to Tribal governments that have a compact under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.9228">3.9228</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) There is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner the amount necessary to make the payments provided in this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.21.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/63/#laws.2.2.0">2023 c 63 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.15.5.0">2023 c 64 art 15 s 5</a>; art 19 s 1</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.20">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.20 DESTRUCTION OF RETURNS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of revenue is hereby authorized to destroy all tax returns, required under chapter 290 or 290A, including audit reports, orders, and correspondence relating thereto, which have been on file in the commissioner's office for a period to be determined by the commissioner. The commissioner may make copies of such returns, orders, or correspondence by microfilm, photostat, or other similar means and may immediately destroy the original documents from which such copies have been made. Such copies, when certified to by the commissioner, shall be admissible in evidence in the same manner and be given the same effect as the original documents destroyed.</p>
        <p>The commissioner may destroy correspondence and documents contained in the files of the division which do not relate specifically to any tax return.</p>
        <p>Notwithstanding the above provisions the commissioner may, utilizing such safeguards as the commissioner in the commissioner's discretion deems necessary, (1) employ a commercial photographer for the purpose of developing microfilm of returns or other documents, or (2) employ a vendor for the purpose of obtaining the vendor's services an example of which is the preparation of income tax return labels.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.20.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/604/#laws.0.27.0">1945 c 604 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/92/#laws.0.1.0">1947 c 92 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/398/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 398 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/120/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 120 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/523/#laws.1.50.0">1982 c 523 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/184/#laws.2.22.0">1989 c 184 art 2 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.21">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.21 TAXPAYER ASSISTANCE GRANTS; TAX CREDIT OUTREACH GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.21.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Taxpayer assistance.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When the commissioner awards grants under this section, the commissioner must provide public notice of the grants in a timely manner so that the grant process is completed and grants are awarded by October 1, in order for recipient organizations to adequately plan expenditures for the filing season. At the time the commissioner provides public notice, the commissioner must also notify organizations that received grants in the previous biennium. Amounts appropriated for grants under this section are not subject to retention of administrative costs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98#stat.16B.98.14">16B.98, subdivision 14</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.21.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Eligible credit" means a credit, refund, or other tax preference targeting low-income taxpayers, including but not limited to the credits under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0661">290.0661</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0671">290.0671</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0674">290.0674</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0693">290.0693</a>, and chapter 290A.</p>
          <p>(c) "Tax outreach organization" means a nonprofit organization or federally recognized Indian Tribe with experience serving demographic groups or geographic regions that have historically had low rates of participation in eligible credits.</p>
          <p>(d) "Taxpayer assistance services" means accounting and tax preparation services provided by volunteers to low-income, elderly, and disadvantaged Minnesota residents to help them file federal and state income tax returns and Minnesota property tax refund claims and to provide personal representation before the Department of Revenue and Internal Revenue Service.</p>
          <p>(e) "Volunteer taxpayer assistance organization" means an eligible organization qualifying under section 7526A(e)(2)(B) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.21.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Taxpayer assistance grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must make grants to one or more volunteer taxpayer assistance organizations to coordinate, facilitate, encourage, and aid in the provision of taxpayer assistance services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.21.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Tax credit outreach grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must make one or more grants to tax outreach organizations and volunteer assistance organizations. Grants provided under this subdivision must be used to:</p>
          <p>(1) publicize and promote the availability of eligible credits to taxpayers likely to be eligible for those credits; or</p>
          <p>(2) provide taxpayer assistance services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.49.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/12/#laws.2.18.0">1Sp2021 c 12 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.71.1.0">2024 c 127 art 71 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.22">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.22 COST OF LIVING ADJUSTMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.22.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Adjustment; definition; period; rounding.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall annually make a cost of living adjustment to the dollar amounts noted in sections that reference this section. The commissioner shall adjust the amounts based on the index as provided in this section. For purposes of this section, "index" means the Chained Consumer Price Index for All Urban Consumers published by the Bureau of Labor Statistics. The values of the index used to determine the adjustments under this section are the latest published values when the Bureau of Labor Statistics publishes the initial value of the index for August of the year preceding the year to which the adjustment applies.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this section, "statutory year" means the year preceding the first year for which dollar amounts are to be adjusted for inflation under sections that reference this section. For adjustments under chapter 290A, the statutory year refers to the year in which a taxpayer's household income used to calculate refunds under chapter 290A was earned and not the year in which refunds are payable. For all other adjustments, the statutory year refers to the taxable year unless otherwise specified.</p>
          <p>(c) To determine the dollar amounts for taxable year 2020, the commissioner shall determine the percentage change in the index for the 12-month period ending on August 31, 2019, and increase each of the unrounded dollar amounts in the sections referencing this section by that percentage change. For each subsequent taxable year, the commissioner shall increase the dollar amounts by the percentage change in the index from August 31 of the year preceding the statutory year to August 31 of the year preceding the taxable year.</p>
          <p>(d) To determine the dollar amounts for refunds payable in 2020 under chapter 290A, the commissioner shall determine the percentage change in the index for the 12-month period ending on August 31, 2019, and increase each of the unrounded dollar amounts in the sections referencing this section by that percentage change. For each subsequent year, the commissioner shall increase the dollar amounts by the percentage change in the index from August 31 of the statutory year to August 31 of the year preceding the year in which refunds are payable.</p>
          <p>(e) Unless otherwise provided, the commissioner shall round the amounts as adjusted to the nearest $10 amount. If an amount ends in $5, the amount is rounded up to the nearest $10 amount.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.22.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Publication.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall announce and publish the adjusted dollar amounts required by subdivision 1 on the Department of Revenue's website on or before December 15 of each year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.22.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Special provision.</span></h2>
          <p>The determination of the commissioner under this subdivision is not a rule and is not subject to the Administrative Procedure Act under chapter 14, including section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.22.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.1.2.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.16.1.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 16 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TAX ADMINISTRATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.25">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.25 PROHIBITION OF SUITS TO RESTRAIN ASSESSMENT OR COLLECTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.25.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General rule.</span></h2>
          <p>No suit to restrain assessment or collection of a tax, fee, penalty, or interest, imposed by a law administered by the commissioner, including a declaratory judgment action, can be maintained in any court by any person except pursuant to the express procedures in (1) this chapter, (2) chapter 271, (3) chapter 289A, and (4) any other law administered by the commissioner for contesting the assessment or collection of taxes, fees, penalties, or interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.25.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Facial challenge to constitutionality.</span></h2>
          <p>An action, otherwise prohibited under subdivision 1, that asserts a facial challenge to the constitutionality of a tax or fee imposed by a law administered by the commissioner may be maintained only if it is demonstrated to the court by clear and convincing evidence that under no circumstances could the commissioner ultimately prevail and that the taxpayer or fee payer will suffer irreparable harm if the relief sought is not granted.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.25.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.22.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 22</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.26">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.26 PENALTY FOR FILING CERTAIN DOCUMENTS AGAINST DEPARTMENT OF REVENUE EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.26.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Recording office" means a county recorder, registrar of titles, or secretary of state in this state or another state.</p>
          <p>(b) "Filing party" means the person or persons requesting or causing another person to request that the recording office accept documents or instruments for recording or filing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.26.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Invalid documents naming commissioner or department employees.</span></h2>
          <p>Filing a document, including a nonconsensual common law lien under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/514.99">514.99</a>, that purports to create a claim against the commissioner or an employee of the department based on performance or nonperformance of duties by the commissioner or employee is invalid unless accompanied by a specific order from a court of competent jurisdiction authorizing the filing of the document or unless a specific statute authorizes the filing of the document.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.26.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Civil penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>If a filing party causes a document described in subdivision 2 to be recorded in a recording office, the commissioner may assess a penalty against the filing party of $1,000 per document filed, payable to the general fund. An order assessing a penalty under this section is reviewable administratively under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.35">270C.35</a> and is appealable to Tax Court under chapter 271. The penalty is collected and paid in the same manner as a tax collected by the commissioner. The penalty is in addition to any other remedy available to the commissioner or to an employee of the department against whom the document has been filed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.23.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 23</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.27">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.27 CIVIL DAMAGES FOR FAILURE TO RELEASE LIEN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.27.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">In general.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A taxpayer may bring a civil action for damages against the commissioner in district court when an employee or the department has knowingly or negligently:</p>
          <p>(1) failed to release a lien as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.63#stat.270C.63.15">270C.63, subdivision 15</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) failed to release a lien within 30 days after satisfaction of the liability on which the lien is based.</p>
          <p>(b) An action under paragraph (a), clause (2), must be preceded by 30 days' written notice by the taxpayer to the commissioner and the taxpayer's rights advocate that the lien has not been released. An action under paragraph (a) must be commenced within two years after the date the right of action accrued.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.27.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Damages.</span></h2>
          <p>On a finding of liability on the part of the defendant in an action brought under subdivision 1, the defendant is liable to the plaintiff in an amount equal to the sum of actual, direct economic damages sustained by the plaintiff due to the actions of the defendant, plus the costs of the action. Damages must be paid in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.736#stat.3.736.7">3.736, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.27.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mitigation of damages.</span></h2>
          <p>Damages awarded must be reduced by the amount of the damages that could reasonably have been mitigated by the plaintiff.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.27.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.11.2.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 11 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.275">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.275 CIVIL DAMAGES FOR CERTAIN UNAUTHORIZED COLLECTION ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.275.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">In general.</span></h2>
          <p>If in connection with the collection of delinquent taxes, an employee of the department recklessly or intentionally disregards a law administered by the commissioner, the taxpayer may bring a civil action for damages against the commissioner in district court within two years after the date the right of action accrues.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.275.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Damages.</span></h2>
          <p>On a finding of liability on the part of the defendant in an action brought under subdivision 1, the defendant is liable to the plaintiff in an amount equal to the lesser of $200,000, or the sum of (1) actual, direct economic damages sustained by the plaintiff as a proximate result of the reckless or intentional actions of the employee and (2) the costs of the action. Damages must be paid in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.736#stat.3.736.7">3.736, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.275.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>A judgment for damages must not be awarded under subdivision 2 unless the court determines that the plaintiff has exhausted the administrative remedies available to the plaintiff within the department. Damages awarded must be reduced by the amount of the damages that could reasonably have been mitigated by the plaintiff.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.275.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalties for procedures instituted primarily for delay.</span></h2>
          <p>When it appears to the district court that:</p>
          <p>(1) proceedings before it under this section have been instituted or maintained by the taxpayer primarily for delay;</p>
          <p>(2) the taxpayer's position in such proceeding is frivolous or groundless; or</p>
          <p>(3) the taxpayer unreasonably failed to pursue available administrative remedies,</p>
          <p class="bl">the district court, in its decision, may require the taxpayer to pay to the department a penalty not in excess of $25,000. The penalty may be collected and paid in the same manner as a tax collected by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.270C.275.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.25.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 25</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.270C.28">
        <h1 class="shn">270C.28 DISCLOSURE OF RIGHTS OF TAXPAYERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.28.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">In general.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prepare statements that set forth in simple and nontechnical terms:</p>
          <p>(1) the rights and obligations of the department and the taxpayer during an audit;</p>
          <p>(2) the procedures by which a taxpayer may appeal an adverse decision of the department, including administrative and judicial appeals;</p>
          <p>(3) the procedures for filing refund claims and filing of taxpayer complaints; and</p>
          <p>(4) the procedures that the department may use in enforcing a law administered by the commissioner, including assessment, jeopardy assessment, levy and distraint, and the filing of liens.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.270C.28.2">
